      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Developments:**
            1. **Sippar has fallen to the Zulus** - Events 35-111 show Sippar was captured, population dropped to 0 then reset to 23, and it was puppeted by The Zulus. This is a major loss.
            2. **Lasers technology** - Was estimated at 6 turns remaining as of the report snapshot, but with 1390 SciencePerTurn, it might be close or already done.
            3. **Global Hegemony vote** - In 9 turns. Zulus have 43 delegates (102% of needed 42). This means if the vote happens as scheduled, Zulus win Diplomatic Victory unless something changes.
            4. **Spaceflight Pioneers policy** - Estimated 10 turns away. Need to check if this is still the case.
            5. **Apollo Program complete** - I have Apollo complete, so Science Victory is theoretically possible if I can build the parts.
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - Sippar lost. Zulu Mechanized Infantry + naval forces are pressing.
            - My military strength: 32,041 vs Zulu 74,443. Very outmatched.
            - I still have 5 core cities: Babylon, Akkad, Dur-Kurigalzu, Nippur, Borsippa.
            - I have Rocket Artillery, Machine Guns, Infantry, Fleet Destroyers, Modern Armor, Special Forces.
            
            **Diplomatic Situation:**
            - Rome: 100/100, Defensive Pact, Order ideology ally. 19 delegates.
            - The Zulus: At war, -100/-100. 43 delegates.
            - Brazil: At war, Zulu vassal. 12 delegates.
            - Huns: At war, Zulu vassal. 14 delegates.
            - Polynesia: Was critical defender, but looking at the report, Polynesia isn't even listed anymore... wait. Let me check. Actually, looking at the Zulu cities, I see Polynesian cities (Samoa, Tonga, Honolulu, Raiatea, Tahiti, Aotearoa, etc.) are now Zulu-occupied. This means Polynesia has capitulated or been defeated. This is disastrous because Polynesia's delegates were preventing Zulu victory.
            
            Wait, looking at the Victory Progress again:
            - Zulus: 43 delegates, 102%
            - Votes needed: 42
            
            But wait - the report says "Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns" and "Proposals: Enact: Global Hegemony".
            
            If Polynesia has fallen, how do Zulus have 43 delegates instead of 46 or more? Let me check... perhaps the delegate count already reflects some losses, or maybe Polynesia isn't fully eliminated but just mostly conquered. Looking at cities: Honolulu is occupied (ID 12082), but is there a Polynesian capital left? I don't see it listed.
            
            Actually, looking at Opponents section, the only major civs listed are Huns (0), Brazil (1), Babylon (2), Zulus (6), Rome (7). No Polynesia listed. But under relationships, I don't see Polynesia either. This suggests Polynesia has been eliminated or is no longer relevant.
            
            But if Zulus have 43 delegates and need 42, they already exceed the threshold. The only question is whether they can maintain this for the vote in 9 turns.
            
            Wait, let me re-read the proposal mechanics: "If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates."
            
            So the Zulus have 43 delegates but need votes, not just delegates. Delegates are voting power. The proposal needs to be voted on. With 43 delegate votes out of... let me calculate total delegates:
            - Babylon: 8
            - Brazil: 12
            - Rome: 19
            - Huns: 14
            - Zulus: 43
            Total: 96 delegates
            
            Zulus have 43/96 = 44.8% of votes. They need 42 votes to win. If they vote for themselves and get their vassals (Brazil 12, Huns 14) to vote for them, that's 43 + 12 + 14 = 69 votes. Wait, can vassals vote against their master?
            
            Actually in Civ 5, vassals usually vote with their master. So Zulus effectively control 69 votes. But they only need 42. So they will definitely win the Global Hegemony vote in 9 turns unless I can somehow prevent the vote or reduce their delegates.
            
            How can I reduce Zulu delegates?
            1. Capture/destroy Zulu cities? Impossible - I can't even hold Sippar.
            2. Capture city-states allied with Zulus? Vatican City (Zulu ally), Ormus (Zulu ally), Vilnius (Zulu ally), Kathmandu (Zulu ally). Oslo is Brazil-allied. Buenos Aires is Zulu friends but not ally.
            3. Liberate Polynesia? That would restore their delegates and subtract from Zulus maybe? But I can't reach Polynesian cities.
            4. Scientists victory before turn 444 (9 turns from now).
            
            Wait, Lasers was estimated at 6 turns at the time of the report. If it's now turn 435, Lasers might complete this turn or next. Then I need:
            - Mobile Tactics (next tech)
            - Then Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization (for spaceship parts)
            
            Actually, looking at the tech tree: the spaceship parts require:
            - Nanotechnology: 1 part
            - Particle Physics: 1 part  
            - Globalization: 1 part? No wait.
            
            Actually in Civ 5, the spaceship parts are:
            - SS Booster (3 needed) - require Nanotechnology? No...
            Wait, let me reconsider. The available technologies listed are:
            - Mobile Tactics
            - The Internet
            
            But the report says: "Completing it [The Internet] would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization"
            
            Wait, that's odd. The Internet unlocking those? No, looking again:
            - Mobile Tactics: Completing it would unlock: Stealth
            - The Internet: Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
            
            This is a Vox Populi mod. In Vox Populi, the tech tree might be different. But regardless, I need those three technologies for the spaceship parts.
            
            Current research is Lasers. After Lasers, I had queued Mobile Tactics. But I need to get to Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Globalization.
            
            Wait, if The Internet is available as a technology option, and it unlocks Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Globalization... The Internet requires Computers, which requires Electronics, which requires Radar, which requires... Actually in Vox Populi the tech tree is reorganized.
            
            Let me think about what's actually available to me. The options show:
            - Mobile Tactics
            - The Internet
            
            These are the only two technologies listed in my options. My current research is Lasers, which is not in the options (because I'm already researching it).
            
            So after Lasers completes, I can choose either Mobile Tactics or The Internet.
            
            Mobile Tactics unlocks Stealth.
            The Internet unlocks Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization.
            
            For Science Victory, I absolutely need Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, and Globalization. The Internet is the gateway to all three.
            
            So I should change my research from Mobile Tactics to The Internet immediately after Lasers completes. Mobile Tactics gives me Mechanized Infantry but at this point, Science Victory is the only way out, and I need those spaceship component technologies ASAP.
            
            Wait, but do I already have something that unlocks them? Let me check my current techs. I'm at 73 technologies. Information era. Lasers almost done.
            
            If The Internet unlocks all three spaceship techs, that's the path I need.
            
            **Policy situation:**
            - Next policy: Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3) in ~10 turns.
            - This gives a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist, and allows finishing spaceship parts with Great Engineers.
            
            But if Global Hegemony vote is in 9 turns, and Spaceflight Pioneers is 10 turns away... I'm cutting it extremely close. Even if I get Spaceflight Pioneers, I still need to build spaceship parts.
            
            Actually, spaceship parts in Vox Populi might work differently. In base Civ 5, you need:
            - SS Cockpit: requires Nanotechnology
            - SS Engine: requires Particle Physics  
            - SS Stasis Chamber: requires Nanotechnology? No...
            
            Wait, in base BNW:
            - SS Booster (3): Advanced Ballistics? No, in BNW it's:
              - 3x SS Booster: Nanotechnology
              - SS Cockpit: Nanotechnology? No...
            Actually in BNW:
            - SS Booster: Advanced Ballistics (2 needed in BNW? No, 3 in G&K)
            Let me reconsider.
            
            Actually for BNW:
            - SS Booster requires Nanotechnology (3 needed)
            - SS Cockpit requires Robotics? No...
            Wait, I should look at this differently. The game is giving me specific options. The key is that I need to research The Internet ASAP because it unlocks the three techs that presumably contain spaceship parts.
            
            Given that the vote is in 9 turns, and I need to:
            1. Finish Lasers (~now or next turn)
            2. Research The Internet (probably ~8-10 turns at 1390 beakers/turn? Actually my science output might change)
            3. Then research Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
            4. Build the parts
            
            This seems impossible in 9 turns. But maybe the vote can be delayed or blocked somehow?
            
            Wait, the proposal mechanics say "May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active."
            
            So Global Hegemony is automatically proposed. It's already proposed and voting in 9 turns. I can't stop it.
            
            If Zulus have 43 delegates and need 42, and they control their vassals, they will win in 9 turns.
            
            Unless... delegates change before then. City-state alliances can flip. But Zulus have so many city-state allies.
            
            Wait, could Rome or I somehow capture Zulu cities and reduce their population/city count? No, Rome's military is weak (5443 strength vs Zulu 74k).
            
            What if I use nuclear weapons? I have Atomic Bombs (events show I rebased them to Nippur). I could potentially nuke Zulu military stacks or cities. But I can't nuke their delegate count directly.
            
            Actually, wait. Vassals might not always vote for their master in World Congress. Let me think. In Vox Populi, vassals usually vote with the master, but it's not always 100% guaranteed, especially if they hate them. But Brazil and Huns are capitulated vassals, so they likely have to.
            
            Is there any other hope? Let me re-examine the delegate count.
            
            Zulus: 43 delegates
            Need: 42
            
            If I could somehow reduce Zulu delegates by 2, they wouldn't have enough for automatic victory. But delegates come from:
            - Their own cities/population
            - City-state allies
            - World Religion (if they have it)
            - World Ideology (currently Autocracy is enacted)
            
            Looking at city-states allied with Zulus:
            - Vatican City: MajorAlly The Zulus
            - Ormus: MajorAlly The Zulus  
            - Vilnius: MajorAlly The Zulus
            - Kathmandu: MajorAlly The Zulus
            
            That's 4 city-states. Each city-state gives delegates. If I could flip even one, that might reduce their count below 42.
            
            But how? I'm at war with them. I can't send diplomats or trade routes to city-states at war with me. And all these city-states are at war with me because they're allied with Zulus.
            
            Wait, can I liberate a city-state from Zulu control? Zulus don't directly control these city-states as cities, they control them as allies.
            
            Actually, looking at the Zulu cities, I see they have many conquered capitals. Do conquered capitals give delegates? In Civ 5 BNW, only original capitals count for Domination, but for World Congress delegates, it's based on city-states allied + population + other factors.
            
            Hmm, let me reconsider whether I can actually win this. The situation seems desperate. Sippar fell. Zulus have overwhelming force. Global Hegemony vote in 9 turns.
            
            What if the vote isn't an automatic loss? Let me re-read:
            "Enact: Global Hegemony: Designate a opponent that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory."
            
            So it's a proposal that needs to pass. But Zulus have 43 delegates themselves, and likely control Brazil (12) and Huns (14). That's 69 votes. The proposal will pass.
            
            Unless... I can kill enough of their units that their warscore changes and something happens? No, that doesn't affect delegates.
            
            Wait, are there any other World Congress mechanics I can use? The current active resolutions include Sanction: The Zulus (not enacted). If that were enacted, would it remove their delegates? No, sanctions don't remove delegates.
            
            What about the Cold War resolution? It prevents trade routes between different ideologies.
            
            Let me think about Science Victory timing again. Maybe it's possible if I'm extremely fast.
            - Lasers: 6 turns remaining at turn 434. So around turn 440.
            - The Internet: Let's say 8-10 turns. So turn 448-450.
            - Then I need 3 more technologies (Nanotech, Particle Physics, Globalization).
            - Global Hegemony vote: turn 444 (9 turns from when? Wait, the report says "Voting in 9 turns" but the report is for turn 434-435. So voting was in ~9 turns from turn 434, meaning turn 443.
            
            Actually at the top under Victory Progress:
            - "Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns"
            
            And this report is after turn 435. Wait, the "Strategies" section says UpdatedTurn 433 for some things, and the current turn is 435. So "Voting in 9 turns" might have been updated. If it's turn 435 and voting is in 9 turns, that's turn 444.
            
            Wait, the Events section ends at turn 435 with "OpponentDoneTurn" and "NextOpponent: 6: The Zulus", but the World Congress status says voting in 9 turns. Let me assume it's 9 turns from the current point (turn 435), so vote at turn 444.
            
            Lasers estimated at 6 turns from turn 434, so completing turn 440.
            The Internet: unknown turns, maybe 6-8 turns. Complete turn 446-448.
            Too late for the vote at turn 444.
            
            But wait, after Lasers completes, I could research The Internet in parallel with... no, it's sequential.
            
            Unless... staff members can be instructed to prioritize Science even more, but I doubt it changes enough.
            
            What about using Great Scientists to bulb techs? I have Great Scientist generation bonuses (Babylon ability). But I don't see any Great Scientists currently available.
            
            Actually, looking at the Espionage section: I have many spies. One spy (Agent Ismi-dagan) was KIA. Others are gathering intelligence. Spy network in Warsaw (Zulu) is 1142, in Raiatea is 970. But spies can't steal technologies that fast.
            
            Wait, let me reconsider the tech path. Is Mobile Tactics really the wrong choice? Mobile Tactics leads to Stealth. Stealth unlocks Stealth Bomber. But The Internet unlocks the three techs I need for spaceship.
            
            Actually, in Vox Populi, the spaceship parts might be unlocked by different techs. But the game explicitly says "The Internet: Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization". And those three sound exactly like the Information era techs that contain spaceship parts in base Civ 5.
            
            So yes, The Internet is mandatory.
            
            Given that Science Victory before turn 444 seems extremely unlikely, what are my options?
            
            1. Prevent Zulus from winning the vote
            2. Survive until next vote
            3. Achieve some other victory
            
            For option 1: How?
            - Delegates needed: 42. Zulus have 43.
            - If Brazil or Huns stop being Zulu vassals, they might not vote for Zulus. But they're capitulated. Can they break free? Vassals can declare war to break free if their master's war weariness is high... but Zulus have 5% war weariness vs me, which is low. Wait, vs Rome it's 8%. Still not high enough.
            - If Rome captures a Zulu city-state ally? City-states are Vatican City, Ormus, Vilnius, Kathmandu. Rome is far from these.
            - If I capture/recapture Sippar before the vote? That doesn't reduce Zulu delegates.
            - Nuke Zulu cities to reduce population? Delegates are partly based on population. But dropping their population significantly enough to lose 2 delegates in 9 turns is impossible with A-bombs.
            - Use my own delegates? I have 8. Rome has 19. Even if we both vote for Rome, that's 27. Zulus have 43. Not enough.
            
            Wait, World Congress proposals can be voted down if "No" gets more votes. So if Zulus propose Global Hegemony for themselves, they need 42 YES votes. If everyone else votes NO, and Zulus and their vassals vote YES... Zulus (43) + Brazil (12) + Huns (14) = 69 YES. Everyone else: Babylon (8) + Rome (19) = 27 NO. The proposal passes.
            
            There's simply no way to stop this vote from passing based on current delegate counts.
            
            Unless... delegates change during the 9 turns. For example:
            - City-state alliances flip
            - Civilizations are eliminated/liberated
            - World Religion or World Ideology changes
            
            Actually, wait. If the Zulus lose a city-state alliance, they lose delegates. If I could somehow flip a Zulu city-state ally, they'd drop below 42.
            
            Looking at Zulu city-state allies:
            - Vatican City: far away, I've never had influence
            - Ormus: at war with me, I have -60 influence
            - Vilnius: at war with me, -60 influence  
            - Kathmandu: at war with me, -60 influence
            
            Because Cold War is active, and these city-states are at war with me (Zulu allies), I can't interact with them. So no chance to flip them.
            
            What about capturing a Zulu city-state and liberating it? If I capture Ormus or Vilnius, I could liberate them, making them a new independent city-state. But they are already city-states... wait, no, these are already city-states. I mean capturing them from Zulu alliance doesn't change anything - they still exist.
            
            Wait! If I capture an original Zulu city, that reduces their base delegates (which come from cities/population). But Zulus have 51 cities. Capturing one won't matter.
            
            Let me reconsider whether I'm reading the delegate count correctly. The report says:
            - The Zulus: Delegates: 43, VictoryPercentage: 102
            
            VictoryPercentage of 102 means they have 102% of needed delegates. They currently exceed the threshold. But do they have 43 delegate votes or 43 delegate power? In World Congress, you need a majority of delegates to vote for you, not just to possess them.
            
            Actually no, looking at the proposal: "If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory." The Zulus need to RECEIVE support from 42 delegates.
            
            They can vote their own 43 delegates for themselves. That's already enough! They don't even need their vassals.
            
            Wait, that changes things! In Civ 5, you can propose yourself for World Leader and vote for yourself. If you have 43 delegates and need 42, you can vote all 43 for yourself and win.
            
            Actually, I recall that in BNW, you cannot propose yourself for World Leader. The proposal is automatic (Global Hegemony). But you can vote for yourself.
            
            So Zulus have 43 delegate votes. They need 42 to win. They can vote 43 for themselves. Game over.
            
            Unless... the delegate count drops below 42 before the vote. How could that happen?
            - Lose a city-state alliance
            - Lose cities/population
            - Lose World Ideology bonus
            
            But none of these seem achievable in 9 turns.
            
            Wait, let me look at the Zulu happiness: 48% (Unhappy - combat penalty). If they became severely unhappy, would they lose delegates? Not directly.
            
            What about a Domination victory? Zulus control 6 of 8 capitals (75%). They need all 8 for Domination. The remaining capitals are:
            - Babylon (me)
            - Rome
            
            If Zulus capture Babylon or Rome, they win Domination instead. But they'd need to capture both our capitals. We're on different continents.
            
            Actually, Rome and I are both Information/Atomic era. We're not completely defenseless.
            
            But Diplomatic Victory is the immediate threat.
            
            Let me reconsider whether the game can be saved. Maybe there's something I'm missing.
            
            Wait - the Zulus have 43 delegates, but the report also says "Votes: 43" under Zulus. And under Diplomatic Victory, it lists delegates for each civ. So yes, they have 43 voting delegates.
            
            Hmm. Actually, is there any mechanic where being at war prevents claiming Diplomatic Victory? No.
            
            Let me re-read the proposal text: "May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active."
            
            So this happens automatically. If no one gets enough support, top 2 get +5 delegates. But Zulus can give themselves enough support.
            
            Wait! I just realized something. The Zulus have 43 delegates, BUT they might not be able to vote all of them for themselves if they have negative relations with other civs? No, in World Congress, each civ distributes their own delegate votes however they want.
            
            Actually, one critical thing: Vassals might not be able to vote independently. In Civ 5, vassals surrender their World Congress votes to their master. Wait, is that true?
            
            In BNW, vassals might actually lose their voting power or have it transferred. Let me think... No, vassals typically vote as their master votes, but they still show as separate vote counts.
            
            But looking at the Zulus: 43 delegates. Brazil: 12 delegates. Huns: 14 delegates. If vassals' votes are controlled by the master, Zulus effectively have 69 votes. But the display shows them separately.
            
            Actually, in BNW World Congress, each civilization casts its own votes. If Brazil and Huns are vassals of Zulus, they usually vote the same way as Zulus. So Zulus effectively control 69 votes.
            
            But even without vassals, Zulus' own 43 votes exceed 42.
            
            Given this seemingly impossible situation, what should my strategy be?
            
            Option A: Accept that Zulus will win Diplomatic in 9 turns. In that case, I should try to win Science Victory BEFORE turn 444. Is that possible?
            
            Current:
            - Lasers: ~6 turns (complete turn 440)
            - Need: The Internet → Nanotechnology → build part, Particle Physics → build part, Globalization → build part.
            - If The Internet is ~8 turns: complete turn 448. Too late.
            - Even if I could somehow instant-research The Internet, the three follow-ups each take time.
            
            Wait, maybe I can use multiple Great Scientists to bulb through multiple techs instantly? In Civ 5, a Great Scientist gives beakers equal to the last 8 turns of science output. At 1390/turn, that's 11,120 beakers per scientist.
            
            But technologies in the Information era cost much more. The Internet probably costs ~15,000-20,000 beakers? Lasers... actually I don't know exact costs.
            
            If I had several Great Scientists saved up, I could bulb multiple techs. But I don't see any Great Scientists in my current Great People list. I do have Great Engineers and other GP from wonders/policies, but no saved scientists.
            
            What about buying Great Scientists with Faith? I have 188 FaithPerTurn. A Great Scientist costs ~1500-2000 faith in the Industrial era, more in later eras. I have 2618 gold, not much faith mentioned in stock (but 188/turn). Could I faith-buy a Great Scientist? In Vox Populi, maybe.
            
            Actually, looking at my cities: Babylon has FaithPerTurn 45, Akkad 45, Dur-Kurigalzu 31, Nippur 23, Borsippa 44. Total ~188. But how much faith do I have saved? Not shown directly.
            
            Even if I could buy a Great Scientist, one won't be enough for multiple techs.
            
            Option B: Go for Culture Victory? I'm influential with 0 civs. Rome is at 99% and will be influential in 1 turn. But that's just 1 civ. I need 4. Impossible in time.
            
            Option C: Domination Victory? Need to control/vassalize all 8 original capitals. Zulus have 6. I need to take all of them plus Rome's? Or am I misunderstanding? To win Domination, I need to be the last one with my original capital, AND control all other original capitals OR vassalize all other civs who still have their capitals. Actually in Vox Populi Domination requires controlling or vassalizing all original capitals. Zulus control 6. Rome and I still have ours. But Zulus are about to win Diplomatic.
            
            Option D: Is there any way to delay the vote?
            
            Actually, thinking more carefully... what if I research The Internet INSTEAD of Mobile Tactics after Lasers? The Internet increases tourism and unlocks the three key techs. It's the only way to even attempt a science victory.
            
            And for policy, Spaceflight Pioneers is still critical. It comes in ~10 turns. But the vote is in ~9 turns.
            
            Wait, what if the "9 turns" is to the next World Congress session, not necessarily the vote executing immediately? And during the session, multiple things happen. But Global Hegemony is the proposal.
            
            Let me re-check: "Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns". This is clear. In 9 turns, voting occurs.
            
            Actually, I realize I might be calculating turns wrong. The report was generated after turn 435. "Voting in 9 turns" means votes are cast at the end of that period? In Civ 5, World Congress meets every 20 turns in Standard speed. Proposals are voted on during the session. The 9 turns might be until the next session starts.
            
            Hmm, but the bottom of the event list shows turn 435 events and then says "NextOpponent: 6: The Zulus" meaning it's now my turn again? Actually no, the event log shows turns 434-435, and ends with my turn completing and next being Zulu.
            
            Wait, the prompt says: "You, Nebuchadnezzar II (leader of Babylon, Opponent 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 435."
            
            So it's the start of a new decision point for me. I need to set strategy for the staff members for the upcoming turns.
            
            Given the near-certain diplomatic loss in ~9 turns, is there ANYTHING I can do?
            
            One thought: In Civ 5, if a player wins Diplomatic Victory, the game ends immediately. So if Zulus get 42 votes on Global Hegemony, it's over.
            
            Unless... wait. Let me re-read the World Ideology: Autocracy description. "Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed."
            
            Was the United Nations constructed? Looking at Zulu cities... Ulundi has "United Nations" wonder! Yes, they built it. So Diplomatic Victory is enabled.
            
            But for the actual victory, they need the Global Hegemony proposal to pass with enough votes.
            
            Hmm. Let me look at this from a different angle. Could I somehow cause the Zulus to lose delegates very quickly?
            
            Looking at the city-state allies again:
            - Kathmandu: 1030 influence for Zulus. Very secure.
            - Ormus: 419 influence. Secure.
            - Vilnius: 754 influence. Secure.
            - Vatican City: 1007 influence. Secure.
            
            All are at war with me. So I can't interact.
            
            Wait - what if I capture these city-states? If I capture a city-state, it's no longer a city-state and its delegate allocation disappears. But capturing a city-state requires taking their city. Ormus and Vatican City are on my side of the map? Let me check coordinates.
            
            Vatican City: 44, 28. That's near Rome/Zulu occupied territory.
            Ormus: 7, 17. That's near my territory! Actually Ormus is at 7,17, and my city Dur-Kurigalzu is at 14,26. Not super close but reachable.
            
            But Ormus has DefenseStrength 124. And I'd have to fight through Zulu forces to get there.
            
            Wait, actually the events show Ormus as being adjacent to contested zones. Looking at Enemy Land Zone 1076: City Ormus. It has friendly posture because there are Rome and Genoa units there. Ormus itself is a Zulu ally but at war with me.
            
            If I somehow captured Ormus, the city-state would be eliminated (since Vox Populi allows conquering city-states? In BNW, city-states cannot be fully conquered without wiping them out, but you can capture their city and make them a puppet? No, city-states can be conquered and become regular cities in Civ 5).
            
            Actually in Civ 5 BNW, if you capture a city-state, you get their city and they cease to exist as a city-state. This would reduce the total number of city-states, which might reduce the total delegates needed? Or reduce Zulu delegates.
            
            But the Huns and Zulus have already eliminated several city-states (Sigtuna, Assur, Warsaw, Seoul, etc.). The delegate threshold adjusts based on the number of civs and city-states. Currently it's 42.
            
            If I capture Ormus and eliminate it, total city-states decrease. VotesNeeded might decrease to 41 or even 40? Let's think... delegates needed depends on number of civilizations and city-states. If city-states are eliminated, needed delegates decrease. That would make it EASIER for Zulus to win, not harder.
            
            So capturing city-states is counterproductive.
            
            What if I liberate a city-state from Zulu control? Looking at cities captured by Zulus... they have captured many major civ cities, but do they have any city-states under their direct control? The city-state list shows independent city-states (Buenos Aires, Vatican City, etc.). Some have been defeated (Sigtuna, Assur, Warsaw, Seoul).
            
            Wait! Warsaw was defeated (ID 26: Defeated Minor Civilization Warsaw). But Warsaw is listed as a city with ID 13481 under Zulu control. That's a city named Warsaw but it's a Zulu city now. Same with Seoul (ID 13225 under Zulus). So city-states that are "defeated" become cities of the conquering civ.
            
            If I could liberate Warsaw or Seoul or Sigtuna, they'd return as city-states. But each liberated city-state would give delegates... actually, liberated city-states might vote for their liberator.
